PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended September 30, 1896. 20A 20
154 CUSTOx S ZETT[JULY--8EPT., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 302. YANGTZE RIVER-CHINKIANG DISTRICT. NORTH TREE BEACON LIGHT. NoTic is hereby given that the North Tree Beacon Light has been shifted 278 yards 8. 860 E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank. SHANGHAI, 21t Jdy 1896. No. 303. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. TUNGSHA LIGHT-V)ESSEL. TEMPORARY FIXED LIGHT REPLACED BY A REVOLVING LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the temporary fixed light of the Tungsha light-vessel Newchwang has been replaced by a revolving light showing white flashes at intervals of half a minute. The light is elevated 35 feet above the sea, and in clear weather it should be visible at a distance of it nautical miles. The light-vessel is painted red, with the word &quot;NEWCHWANG&quot; in white on each side, and she has three masts, the main carrying a black ball. During thick or foggy weather a steam fog horn will be sounded at intervals of 1o seconds. SANGHMI, 3rd September 1896.
1896.] NOTrES TO MARINERS. 10 No. 304. YANGTZE RIVER-KIUKIANG DI.STRICT. TWO-FATHOM CREEK BEACON LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Two-fathom Creek Beacon Light has been shifted 270 yards N. 420 E. from its last position, in consequence of the subsidence of the land on which it stood. SHANGHAI, 22nd September I896. No. 305. YANGTZE RIVER-KIUKIANG DISTRICT. N.E. CROSSING CHANNEL AND SPENCER ROCK LIGHT-BOATS. NOTICE is hereby given that the lights of the light-boats marking the N.E Crossing Channel and Spencer Rock have been changed from white to red. SHANGHAI, 22nd September x896. 7-I

70 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JULY-SEPT., (Received 19th October 1896.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 13Ta OCTOBER 1896. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring ations in Native vessels for the third quarter of the year 1896, July-September. b. The Custom House (A ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin nd Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two 4wang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.Td 30 and fkM So per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present ntrolled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon (t * ), Cbangcbow ( b ), Fotochow )A , and Kowloon (jL 4E) statibnas. c. The word &quot; Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II. M. HILLIER, Commissioner of Customs. To the INSPECTOR GENERAL OF CUSTOMS.
(Receiaved 17th October 1896.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, TOTH OCTOBER 1896. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the third quarter of the year 1896, July-Septendber. b. The Custom House ( k ) was opened on the 2nd April . The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.yh 3o and Hk.2h 80 per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-4, Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- mnenced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the Tat July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lapp&amp;amp; Customs are known as the Malowchow (4 $ ) and Chienshan (i 1 [1) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (W 4), Kuttai (W *), and Kwanchiap (U ) sub-statbus. c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. 1l.
